Codebook

How each paper builds the variables it uses: the rule, the threshold, the register and, where the authors state them, the exact register variable names and code values. Definitions come from replication code where it is public, otherwise from online appendices, working-paper appendices or the paper itself, and each one links to its source.

1129 definitions from 150 papers. By source: 689 from working paper, 266 from replication code, 126 from paper, 48 from online appendix. Register variable names and codes change between years and projects, so check the current Statistics Denmark documentation before reusing a code list. AEA replication packages sit on openICPSR, which could not be read for this page, so AEA papers rely on their appendices.

Concept

Adult SES indexBaker et al. (2026)Row mean of years of education, higher education, average earnings 30-50 and average employment 30-50, each standardised by the control-group (not born days 1-3) mean and SD, set to missing if any component is missing, then rescaled to control-group SD 1. A labour market index (earnings at 25, average earnings 30-50, average employment) and an education index are built the same way.UDDA, IND, IDAP

Annual earnings (register)Caplin et al. (2026)Annual earnings are wage income LOENMV_13 from the income register (IND); self-employment income is NETOVSKUD_13 kept separately. Realized earnings growth 2020-2021 is ln(earnings_2021*100/102) - ln(earnings_2020), dropping observations with earnings below 24,000 DKK in either year and trimming at the 1st/99th percentile.IND

Annual earnings eight years after applicationHeinesen et al. (2026)Annual earnings eight years after the first observed application: wage income, self-employment income and transfers replacing such income (short-term sickness pay, paid parental leave), excluding unemployment benefits; deflated by CPI (base 2011) and converted to 1,000s of USD at 2010-2016 average rates (5.9 DKK per USD).Income registersworking paper · Section 5.2 'Data and descriptive statistics'
Annual earnings incl. self-employment (robustness)Jensen and Blundell (2024)Annual income measures from the income register IND, used in a robustness check that includes the self-employed because self-employment income is not measured monthly.INDpaper · Section 5.1
Annual gross earningsHasager and Jørgensen (2026)Annual gross earnings from tax returns in the Income Register, CPI-deflated to 2000 and converted to USD at the 27 March 2020 exchange rate.INDworking paper · Section I
Annual gross labour earningsFoged et al. (2024)Annual gross labour market income from the income register (IND), converted to US dollars at 2015 prices; short/medium/long-run outcomes are individual averages over years 1-5, 6-10 and 11-15 since asylum. Monthly version (ALMP) multiplied by 12.INDworking paper · Section 4.1, footnote 9; Section 5.2
Annual income (November job)Traiberman (2019)Total salary-based income reported by IDA in November of each year, covering the worker's primary and any secondary occupation; all of it is attributed to the reported (primary) occupation. Hours are not used because they are often imputed. Deflated by the 2000 Danish CPI (1000s DKK).IDAonline appendix · Online Appendix D.1, Income; notes to Table A.7
Annual labour earningsGarcía-Miralles and Leganza (2024)Taxable annual labour market earnings from the annual registers, winsorized at the 1st and 99th percentiles, deflated to 2010 prices and converted at 1 USD = 5.56 DKK.Annual income registersworking paper · Section 3.2
Annual labour earningsBingley and Cappellari (2019)Annual pre-tax labour earnings from income tax returns, summed over all employments in the year, 1980-2014, deflated to 2012 DKK with a year-specific index (earn/R*6768). In code: IDA person variable lonind for 1980-2009, corloen for 2010-2011 and from the IND register for 2012-2014; negative values recoded to 0.IDA (idaperson), IND, Income Statistics Register

Annual labour incomeBingley et al. (2020)Annual labour income from employment and self-employment, including sickness benefits and paternity leave benefits, from employer reports to the tax authorities (sum over all employments in the calendar year); excludes occupational pension contributions and study grants/loans. Measured at ages 25-35 and used in logs (zeros drop out); sensitivity measure excludes sickness and leave benefits.Employer income reports to tax authoritiesworking paper · Section III (Data), footnotes 14, 16
Annual labour income (BFL)Jakobsen et al. (2026)12-month sum of monthly labour income from BFL pay slips, by calendar year for age profiles and centred on the birth month for event-study moments (e.g. income from January-February before a March birth counts as year -1); excludes income registered while on parental leave; deflated by CPI, in 1000 DKK. Estimation sample 2010-2018.BFL (eIndkomst), OFworking paper · Section 2; Supplemental Material A
Annual labour market income (earnings at 30)Jensen and Manning (2025)Aggregate labour market income including wage income and profits from self-employment in the year the child turns 30, in 2013 prices (CPI-deflated). Also used as an income rank, IHS and log.INDonline appendix · Online Appendix OA.1; paper Section III
Annualized earningsKleven et al. (2014)Earnings of part-year residents are annualized based on duration of stay in the year, with duration of stay measured from the migration database (daily dates of entry and exit from CPR records).Administrative tax records, CPR migration recordsworking paper · Section 4.1, footnote 23
Broad income (BI)Jakobsen and Søgaard (2022)Labour market income plus transfers, certain pension payouts and received alimony; it equals personal income before deductions.Danish tax recordspaper · Table 1; Fig. 3 notes
Capital income (CI), positive and negativeKleven and Schultz (2014)Interest income, rental income and business capital income minus interest on debt (mortgage, bank loans, credit cards, student loans), excluding stock income; analysed separately as positive and negative capital income because they are taxed very differently.Income Tax Registerpaper · Table 1; Section I; footnote 6
Child support paymentRossin-Slater and Wüst (2018)Tax-deductible annual child support payments by non-custodial fathers as reported to the tax authorities, so only payments above the non-deductible extra amount are observed. Formal and voluntary payments cannot be distinguished.Danish income/tax registersworking paper · Section 4 (Outcomes)
Cumulative normalized earningsUtar (2018)Sum over 2002-2010 of annual primary-job (November) earnings deflated by CPI (2010=1), divided by the worker's pre-shock average earnings (normsal9699, the 1996-1999 average in the code; the 2015 working paper says the 1999-2000 average). Cumulative hours, total wages and personal income are built the same way.IDA
rsalary (joblon, primary November job earnings); rtotwag (slon, total wages from all jobs); rpersonindk (personal income); CInc=sum(rsalary*Dum0210)/normsal		replication code · DataPrep.do ('Preparation of Cumulative variables'); ReadMe File.docx
Deceased's income share (instrument)Fadlon et al. (2026)Share of overall household income earned by the deceased spouse in the baseline year r = -1; used to instrument household income loss.Income Registry (IND)working paper · Section I (Variable definitions); Section II.B
Disposable incomeEshaghnia et al. (2025)Total personal income (wage income, business and self-employment income, capital income, public transfer income, property income and other non-classifiable personal income) plus the rental value of own home for owner-occupiers, minus taxes and interest expenses. Spouses' or cohabitees' resources pooled where appropriate.INDworking paper · Section 3.2, measure 2 and footnote 18
Earned incomeMaire and Schjerning (2013)Income before shifting: personal income plus retained earnings plus income shifted along other margins such as pension contributions and transfers to assisting spouses; used (adjusted for income shocks) to estimate the real bunching response.Income Tax Registerworking paper · Section 4 discussion of Figures 11-12

Earnings, disposable income and employmentDustmann et al. (2024)Earnings = wage earnings plus self-employment income and business profits, pre-tax; disposable income = pre-tax earnings plus public transfers minus taxes (2020 PPP USD). Excess income and predicted paybackSøgaard (2019)Total earned income from tax records minus the individual income limit; positive amounts translate into payback of benefits. Predicted end-of-year benefits (initial payout minus predicted payback) match reported benefits within DKK 2 for 97.9% of the core sample.Tax returns, Student Benefit Administration recordsworking paper · Section 4.3; Appendix C, Table C.I

Gross hourly wageBingley et al. (2013)Calculated by Statistics Denmark as annual labour earnings (reported by employers to the tax authorities) divided by hours worked, where hours are an annual average derived by Statistics Denmark from mandatory pension (ATP) contributions adjusted for contribution rates by sector and collective agreement. Zero wages dropped, bottom and top 0.5% trimmed per year, observations below minimum wage dropped.Statistics Denmark administrative registers, ATPworking paper · Section 4 and footnote 13
Hourly wageJensen and Manning (2025)Hourly wage from monthly hours and monthly earnings for employees in the monthly register (available from 2008; self-employed excluded), in 2013 prices. Jobs are excluded if the hourly wage is above 5,000 or below 30 DKK, annual hours are above 3,500 or below 7.4, or total wages are above 10,000,000 or below 1,000 DKK.IDANonline appendix · Online Appendix OA.1
Hourly wageLabanca and Pozzoli (2022)Annual earnings divided by the sum of regular and overtime hours.IDA, LONworking paper · Section 3

Hourly wage rateDahl et al. (2013)Sum of total labour income (from tax files in the Income Register) and mandatory pension fund payments, divided by the total number of hours worked in the year (ATP-imputed); observations with unobserved wage, wage flagged by Statistics Denmark as low precision, or in the top or bottom half percentile are discarded.IND, IDA, ATPworking paper · Section 4 (pp. 13-14)
Hourly wage rate (IDA)Kreiner et al. (2015)Annual labour income (including mandatory pension fund payments) divided by annual hours; annual hours are based on the mandatory ATP pension contributions, so overtime is not counted and part-time hours are imprecise.IDAworking paper · Section 3.1; Section 5 (robustness)
Hours-adjusted wage rate (WSR imputation)Kreiner et al. (2015)Robustness check: the employer-reported hourly wage from the Wage Statistics Register (Lønstatistikken) is regressed on the IDA wage rate after dropping the top and bottom 1% and IDA wages flagged as low quality by Statistics Denmark. The fitted relationship is then used to impute an overtime- and part-time-adjusted wage for all workers.IDA, Lønstatistikken (WSR)working paper · Section 5 (robustness), Figure 5
Household disposable incomeBrowning et al. (2013)Gross income including interest income, housing transfers and dividends, minus taxes on income, taxes on shares, surplus on owner-occupied housing and tax-exempted interest expenses (labour, transfer and capital income net of personal taxes).Income tax registersworking paper · Section 4 (Expenditure imputation)
Household disposable incomeBrowning and Leth-Petersen (2003)Sum of gross income including interest income, housing transfers and dividends from share capital, minus taxes on income, taxes on shares, surplus on owner-occupied housing and tax-exempted interest expenses.Income tax registersworking paper · Section 4 (Expenditure imputation) - restatement of Browning and Leth-Petersen (2003)
Household disposable income (bank inflows) and componentsAndersen et al. (2023)Sum of monthly external inflows to accounts of the affected person and spouse: direct deposits, person-to-person transfers from outside the household, and cash deposits. Split into salary (inflows from a bank registration number mapped to the person's employer via payroll data, or salary type codes; joint-account salary attributed to spouse if from spouse's employer), government transfers (registration number linked to a paying agency, or the central-government number for non-central-government workers), and a residual of private transfers and other inflows.Danske Bank transaction data, Monthly payroll and transfer recordsworking paper · Appendices A and C
Household income (net of transfers tied to age/pension)Dahl et al. (2024)Individual income = total personal income PERINDKIALT minus public pensions (QPENSNY), cash assistance (KONTHJ/KONT_GL) and other transfers (ANDOVERFORSEL), with negative business income, negative capital income (after removing surplus from owner-occupied housing OVSKEJD02_NY/OVSKEJD07) and negative wage components added back; household income is the mean of own and spouse/cohabiting partner income (rowmean of inc and partner inc).IND (INDUPD), BEF, FAIN

Income conceptsChetty et al. (2014)Labor income = labor earnings before taxes excluding contributions to employer-administered pensions; total compensation = labor earnings plus employer and employee contributions to employer-administered accounts; taxable income = labor income plus other income (e.g., UI) affecting tax liability. Income measures and public expenditureDustmann et al. (2024)From the income register, from the first year post residency: pre-tax labor earnings (zero if none), pre-tax transfer income, pre-tax gross income (earnings plus transfers) and post-tax disposable income (gross income minus tax payments); public expenditure is transfers minus tax payments. Income-shifting indicatorKreiner et al. (2016)Equals one if income in a month is high (more than 50 percent above) relative to its level in the same month of 2008 and income in the preceding month is low (more than 50 percent below) relative to its 2008 level; zero otherwise. Robustness with 25%-25% and 75%-75% cutoffs; a stricter version also requires November 2009 income to be unusually low.eIncome register (SKAT)working paper · Section III.A, eq. (3) and footnote 6
Indexed earningsJensen and Zhang (2026)Earnings from employment and self-employment, inflation-adjusted to 2020 levels, winsorized at 98%, and indexed by the average earnings of men and women (separately) one year before parental death so effects read as percent of the baseline mean.IND, AKMworking paper · Section III.A and footnote 8
Initial income bin dummiesJakobsen and Søgaard (2022)Fifty dummies for initial personal income measured at the start of each period (e.g. 2004 or 2008). Household marginal interest rate (2008)Kreiner et al. (2019)Computed at household level (respondent linked to spouse/partner) from SKAT account-level deposit and loan files: account-specific rate = 2008 interest payments on account l divided by the average of end-2007 and end-2008 balances. Mortgage loans are excluded in reported results.SKAT account-level deposit and loan records 2007-2009working paper · Section 4.2

Imputed household consumptionGiorgi et al. (2019)Annual household consumption (spending) imputed from the budget constraint as C = Y - T - dA, where Y = gross income from all sources (labour and capital) + housing support + child support - implicit tax on the consumption value of owned housing, T = total taxes paid, and dA = change in assets (cash, bank deposits, stocks and shares, property value, cars and other vehicles, minus liabilities). Imputed spendingJensen and Johannesen (2017)Spending = disposable income minus change in net wealth. Unhedged Interest Rate Exposure (URE)Crawley and Kuchler (2023)Difference between all maturing assets (including income) and maturing liabilities (including planned consumption), where maturing means due for an interest rate reset. 225-hour work requirement / sanctionCairo and Mahlstedt (2023)After 12 months on welfare benefits within the last three years, recipients keep the full benefit only if they worked at least 225 hours in regular non-subsidised jobs in the previous 12 months. Otherwise the benefit is reduced by about DKK 500-1,000 per month depending on family status; after a sanction the hour count resets to zero. Caseworkers may grant exemptions to those unable to work at least five hours a week.Public employment service data (sanctions, exemptions, collected hours)working paper · Section 2

UI spellGeerdsen (2006)Unemployment spell = at least 15 days of unemployment within a given month; a spell is broken if the individual receives no UI for more than 2 weeks in a month or regains the right to a new UI period mid-spell. Exit is any departure from UI (employment, leave, social benefits, early retirement, disability pension, education) in the thesis version. UI days per month come from SSHS, built from CRAM (unemployment fund records).SSHS, CRAMworking paper · PhD thesis (SFI 2003), Paper II, Sections 5.1-5.2, pp. 113-116

Group (gang) crime convictionDustmann et al. (2023)A conviction for a gang-type offence in a case where more than one person is convicted (gang_crime_3 requires at least three). Gang-type offences are non-sexual violence, arson/robbery-type property offences, drug offences and weapons offences; a case is gang-type if anyone in the case is convicted of a gang-type offence.KRAF
gang type: 2-digit AFG_GER7 (floor(ger7/100000)) == 12; 4-digit (floor(ger7/1000)) in 1312, 1339, 1380, 1390, 1435, 1440, 3410; 7-digit in 1366505, 1445750, 1445777-1445782, 3210510; gang_crime = N convicted in journr > 1 & gang type		replication code · do/build/clean/convictions.do
Guilty and dropped/not-guilty chargesJensen and Manning (2025)Counts by end of the year turning 30 of guilty charges and of charges dropped without conditions or ending in a not-guilty verdict (following the Danish Institute for Human Rights 2022). The share dropped/not guilty is computed among those with at least one charge.KRSI, KRKO, KRAF

Effective marginal tax / phase-out rateSøgaard (2019)Marginal income tax of 42% above a personal allowance of DKK 49,900. Benefits are phased out at 50% for the first DKK 11,600 above the individual income limit and at 100% beyond that, so the effective marginal rate jumps from 42% to 72% at the baseline limit under optimal take-up. If the payback exceeds DKK 9,200, the whole payback rises by 7% (a notch). The 2009 reform raised the baseline limit by 24% and the phase-out rate from 52% to 62%.working paper · Section 3.1, footnotes 5 and 11, Figure 2
Effective marginal tax rateKreiner et al. (2015)Effective marginal tax rates by annual labour income from the Kleven and Kreiner (2006) empirical tax function for 2006 (income taxes, indirect taxes and benefit claw-back), deflated to 2005 by average labour income growth; hourly wages are converted to annual income using median annual hours of 1,660. The participation tax rate m0 is 0.59 at baseline (0.82 in a robustness check).working paper · Section 3.1, Figure 1; Section 5

High- vs low-income workersLabanca and Pozzoli (2022)Low-income: tax-exempt or in the bottom tax bracket in 2008. Household tax bracket / treatment statusGruber et al. Marginal tax rate on labour incomeSigaard (2023)Simulated using tax programs from Kleven and Schultz (2014), extended by the Danish Ministry of Finance (available 1984-2016), as [T_t(z+100) - T_t(z)]/100, with income information mainly from IND, INDK and INPI. Mechanical change in net-of-tax rate (instrument)Kleven and Schultz (2014)log(1 - tau_{s+3}(z_s)) - log(1 - tau_s(z_s)): change in the marginal net-of-tax rate simulated by applying year s+3 tax law to base-year s incomes; analogous mechanical change in virtual income instruments for the change in log virtual income.Income Tax Registerpaper · Section III.B
Mechanical net-of-tax rate change (instrument)Jakobsen et al. (2026)Four-year mechanical change log(1 - tau_{t+4}(z_t)) - log(1 - tau_t(z_t)) in the marginal net-of-tax rate, holding income and characteristics at year-t values, computed with a Danish tax simulator; used to instrument own and partner net-of-tax rate changes and virtual income. Mechanical net-of-tax rate change (instrument)Labanca and Pozzoli (2022)Post-reform (2011) marginal tax rate simulated using pre-reform (2008) income adjusted for inflation; the log change in the marginal net-of-tax rate from this mechanical rate instruments the observed change. Net-of-tax interest rateGruber et al. (2021)(1 - tau) R, where tau is the marginal tax rate on negative capital income; the 1987 reform set this rate to 51 percent in all brackets (pre-reform top about 73, middle 62, bottom 48 percent in the average municipality), raising the net-of-tax rate by 81 percent for the top group, 29 percent for the middle and reducing it 6 percent for the bottom.working paper · Section 3.1, Figure 1

Predicted (mechanical) change in net-of-tax rate instrumentSigaard (2023)ln(1 - T'_{t+s}(z_{i,t-k})) - ln(1 - T'_t(z_{i,t-k})): marginal rates under the period t+s and t tax systems evaluated at income lagged k periods, following Weber (2014). Random-audit detected evasionAlstadsæter et al. (2019)SKAT stratified random audits for tax years 2008, 2010 and 2012 (18,985 taxpayers; self-employed and complex returns oversampled), every line item examined; main results include all errors; deliberate evaders are those rated 0-2 on SKAT's 0-6 compliance scale. Evasion rate = taxes evaded / (taxes paid + taxes evaded) by wealth group, weighted by audit weights.SKAT random audit data
SKAT compliance rating 0-2 = deliberate evasion		online appendix · Section 4 (Random Audit Data); Online Appendix H.1-H.2

Scheme earnings eligibility thresholdKleven et al. (2014)Foreigners' scheme requires annual wage earnings with the requesting employer above a threshold that grows roughly with average earnings and lies between the 99.2th and 99.4th percentile of the Danish wage earnings distribution (DKK 765,600 in 2009); it must be met on an annualized basis (e.g. a 6-month contract needs half the annual threshold). For pre-1991 years the threshold is imputed by holding the threshold-to-average-earnings ratio at its 1991 value, with average earnings computed over all workers with positive earnings.Administrative tax and payroll recordsworking paper · Section 2.1 and footnote 14

Simulated marginal tax rateLabanca and Pozzoli (2022)Marginal tax rate on labour income simulated from the Danish Tax Register with an extension of the Kleven-Schultz (2014) tax simulator to 2006-2011: increase in tax liability from raising labour income by 100 DKK, holding other income components fixed. Simulated marginal tax rateKleven and Schultz (2014)Marginal tax rate on income component j computed with the authors' own Danish tax simulator (1984-2005) as [T(z_j + 100) - T(z)]/100, i.e. by raising that component by DKK 100; accounts for spousal jointness of exemptions and the marginal tax ceiling.Income Tax Registerpaper · Section II; footnote 9

Tax schedule kinksMaire and Schjerning (2013)Piecewise-linear personal income tax with five brackets before 1996 and four from 1996; e.g. 2001: about 8% below 33,400 DKK, about 44% up to 179,900 DKK (bottom tax), 49% above (middle tax) and 63% above 279,900 DKK (top tax). Top-tax base is mainly individual; middle-tax base depends on household income; middle cutoff raised to the top cutoff in 2009.Income Tax Registerworking paper · Section 2

Sample restricted to singles; main cross-section pools 2014-2018 (2014-2016 for bank data).BEF, INDpaper · Section I.C (Sample); Figure 1 notes (panel C)
Treatment assignment and study populationSimonsen and Skipper (2025)Work units (314 units of eldercare workers across 110 employers in 11 municipalities) were randomised in September 2008, with 144 units (46%) assigned to Healthy-at-Work. The population is individuals employed at participating work units in the month just before randomisation (7,541 of 7,660 linked to registers). Treatment status is fixed at the initial randomisation month, so post-randomisation moves are ignored and estimates are intention-to-treat.Healthy-at-Work experimental records, Statistics Denmark registers (CPR-linked)working paper · Sections 2.1, 3 and 4 (treatment assignment)
Treatment/control (Fadlon-Nielsen DiD)Andersen et al. (2026)Alternative design comparing individuals diagnosed in a given year with those diagnosed Delta years later (Delta from 3 to 8), with person, year-by-age, in-prison and cancer-recurrence fixed effects. The baseline sample keeps only the 10 years before cancer for pre-diagnosis observations.Cancer register, Crime registersonline appendix · Online Appendix H; Appendix A
Treatment/control by event timingFadlon et al. (2026)Treatment: spouse has a fatal event in year tau (1999-2013); control: spouse has a fatal event in tau + 5 (2004-2018) and is assigned a placebo event in tau (Delta = 5, robustness 3-7). Couples defined by marriage or cohabitation five years before the event; both spouses Danish residents aged 45-80 in the event year; self-employed excluded; sample balanced from four years before to four years after.BEF, LPR, Death Registry, INDworking paper · Section II.A (Quasi-Experiment, Analysis Sample); Appendix Table B.1 notes
Treatment: born after municipal program implementationHjort et al. (2017)Indicator for being born in a municipality after it implemented the home visiting program (exact implementation dates 1937-1949 from the Danish National Archives), assigned from date and parish of birth; parishes mapped to municipalities using the 1930, 1940 and 1950 DigDag parish/municipality structures. Implementation assumed permanent once adopted.Administrative population data (date and parish of birth), Danish National Archives, DigDagpaper · Section III.A, footnote 34; Section II footnote 17
Treatment: born on days 1-3 of the monthBaker et al. (2026)Indicator equal to 1 if the child's day of birth (from the transcribed nurse records) is 1-3 and 0 if 4-31; this identifies eligibility for the extended toddler home-visiting trial.Copenhagen nurse records, CPR
born13ext = 1 if BirthDateDay in 1-3; 0 if 4-31 (born13)		replication code · dst_replicationpackage/do/1b_cr_format_ext_data.do; 1d_cr_merge_ext_data_no_outcomes.do
Unemployment spell inflow sampleMunch et al. (2006)Flow sample of all unemployment spells in 1997-2000 for individuals in a 2.5% random sample of the Danish population, restricted to inflow into unemployment of workers aged 19-66; spell duration measured in weeks (18,992 persons, 52,738 spells).Administrative registers (2.5% random population sample)working paper · Section 3 (Data and the Danish labour and housing markets), pp. 9-10
VERP participant analysis sampleGarcía-Miralles and Leganza (2024)Danes born within six months of 1 January 1954 who made VERP participatory contributions in at least 70 percent of pre-announcement years 2001-2010, balanced over 2006-2018, excluding the self-employed (defined pre-announcement); 40,042 individuals (12,020 within 56 days for RD).Population registers, VERP contribution recordsworking paper · Section 4.2
VLBW treatment / running variableDaysal et al. (2022)Focal child's birth weight (bw) centred at 1,500 grams; treatment indicator vlbw = 1 if birth weight < 1,500 g; local linear RD with separate slopes on each side and triangular weights 1-|bw-1500|/bandwidth.MFR
vlbw = (bw < 1500); f1 = (bw-1500)*vlbw; f2 = (bw-1500)*(1-vlbw)		replication code · 09 Final data preparation and analyses.do, lines ~568-574
Wage-effect repeated cross-section sampleKleven et al. (2014)For year t, foreigners who arrived in Denmark in year t-1 and stayed the full calendar year t; earnings between 70-95% (below) and 105-400% (above) of the threshold, excluding 95-105% (bunchers) and above 400%; years 1991-1994 excluded because scheme earnings are not computerized.Administrative tax records, CPR migration records, Scheme beneficiary recordsworking paper · Section 4.2, Figure 8, Table 3, footnote 30
Working-age native samplesFoged and Peri (2016)Working-age population: aged 18-65, not in school and not permanently disabled. Panel regressions use Danish-born individuals employed in November (IDA recorded last week of November); DiD uses a balanced panel of natives aged 21-51 in 1994, followed 1991-2008 unless disabled, emigrated or died. Low skilled = without college education.IDAworking paper · Section 3.1

(2016)From a January 2011 telephone survey of a random adult sample merged to the eIncome register (878 taxpayers): aware if the respondent answered both that extra income is taxed lower 'after 1 January' and that postponing payout from 2009 to 2010 is 'legal'.Telephone survey, eIncome register (SKAT)working paper · Section III.C, Table 4
Car types (green/brown x heavy/light)Gillingham et al. (2022)Cars are split by whether weight is above or below the median for the car's vintage cohort, then within each half by whether fuel efficiency is above (green) or below (brown) the median, giving four types; attributes are averaged within type. Cars are grouped into 25 age groups from new to 24+ years (cars over 22 years dropped from estimation).Car ownership register (Statistics Denmark)working paper · Appendix F.1 (Car types); Section 6.2
